The p p -adic El Zein-Steenbrink-Zucker bifiltered complex of a projective SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD
Yukiyoshi Nakkajima

Abstract




Abstract.—

Let
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
be a
p
-adic PD-formal family of log points and
let
S_{0}
be an exact closed log subscheme defined by
{\cal I}
.
For a proper SNCL scheme
X/S_{0}
with a relative SNCD
D
on

X/S_{0}
with structural morphism

f\colon X\longrightarrow S_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
,
we construct a
p
-adic bifiltered complex
(A((X,D)/S),P^{D},P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{2}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
.
In the case where
\overset{\circ}{X}
is projective over
\overset{\circ}{S}
, we give the log
p
-adic
relative monodromy-weight conjecture relative to the induced filtration of
P^{D}

on the log crystalline cohomological sheaf
R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})

of
(X,D)/S

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
That is, we conjecture that, if
\overset{\circ}{X}
is projective over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
,
then the log
p
-adic relative monodromy filtration on

R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
relative to
the induced filtration of
P^{D}
exists
and it is equal to the induced filtration of
P

on
R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
We prove that, if the log
p
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture for
D^{(k)}/S

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
is true,
then the log
p
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture is true for
(X,D)/S
.
In particular, we prove the log
p
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture
in the case where the relative dimension of
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}

is less than or equal to
2
or the case where,
for each connected component
S^{\prime}_{1}
of
S_{1}
, there exists an exact closed point
s\in S^{\prime}_{1}

such that the fiber
(X_{s},D_{s})/s
of
(X,D)/S_{1}
at
s
is the log special fiber
of a proper strict semistable family over a complete discrete valuation ring
of equal characteristic.

E_{2}
-degeneration of the
p
-adic weight spectral sequence

12
. Strict compatibility

§
13
. Log
p
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture

References

1
Introduction

The aim of this paper is to give the formulation of
the log
p
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture.
In
[
NS
]
we have constructed the weight filtration on
the log crystalline cohomologcal sheaf
of a proper smooth scheme with a relative SNCD(=simple normal crossing divisor);
in
[
N4
]
and
[
N5
]
we have constructed the weight filtration on
the log crystalline cohomologcal sheaf of
a proper SNCL(=simple normal crossing log) scheme) in characteristic
p>0
.
This paper is a continuation of papers
[
NS
]
and
[
N4
]
.
In this paper we construct the weight filtration on the log crystalline cohomological sheaf
of a proper SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD in characteristic
p>0
.
This paper is a generalization of
[
NS
]
and
[
N4
]
at the same time.

On the other hand, in
[
SZ
]
Steenbrink and Zucker have
constructed a bifiltered complex for a
proper strict semistable family with a relative SNCD over the unit disk over
{\mathbb{C}}
.
In
[
E
]
El Zein has also constructed the same bifiltered complex.
In
[
N6
]
we construct the theory of the weight filtration on the
l
-adic Kummer log étale
cohomological sheaf of a proper SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD in any characteristic
inspired by their work. Because the weight filtration should be motivic, we construct
the
p
-adic analogue of the weight filtration on
[
N6
]
in this paper.

In order to state our main result in this paper, let us first recall a result in
[
N4
]
.

For a log (formal) scheme
Y
, denote by
\overset{\circ}{Y}
and

M_{Y}=(M_{Y},\alpha_{Y}\colon M_{Y}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{Y})

the underlying (formal) scheme of
Y
and the log structure of
Y
, respectively.
Let
S
be a
p
-adic formal family of log points defined in
[
N4
]
;
locally on
S
,
S
is isomorphic to a log
p
-adic formal scheme

(\overset{\circ}{S},{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S})
,
where the morphism
{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S}

is defined by the morphism

(n,a)\longmapsto 0^{n}a

(n\in{\mathbb{N}},a\in{\cal O}_{S}^{*})
, where
0^{n}=0\in{\cal O}_{S}

for
n\not=0
and
0^{0}:=1\in{\cal O}_{S}
.
Let
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
be a
p
-adic formal PD-family of log points
(
S
is a
p
-adic formal family of log points and

{\cal I}
is a quasi-coherent
p
-adic PD-ideal sheaf of
{\cal O}_{S}

with PD-structure
\gamma
).
Let
S_{0}
be an exact closed log subscheme of
S
defined by
{\cal I}
.
Let
X/S_{0}
be a proper SNCL scheme with structural morphism

f\colon X\longrightarrow S_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
.
(In §
2
below we recall the definition of the SNCL scheme briefly.)
Let
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be
the set of smooth components of
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
defined in
[
N4
]
.
(When
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
is the spectrum of a field of characteristic
p>0
,

\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
can be taken as
the set of the irreducible components of
\overset{\circ}{X}
.)
For a nonnegative integer
k
, let

(1.0.1)

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}:=\coprod_{\{\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{k}\}~|\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda,\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}(i\not=j)\}}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{k}}

be a scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
well-defined in
[
N4
]
.
Let
a^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
be the natural morphism.
Let
F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}\colon\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
be the absolute Frobenius
endomorphism of
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
and
set
S_{0}^{[p]}:=S_{0}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
.
Let

F_{S_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}\colon S_{0}\longrightarrow S_{0}^{[p]}

be the relative Frobenius morphism of
S_{0}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
.
Let
S_{0}^{[p]}(S)
be a log formal scheme whose underlying formal scheme
is
\overset{\circ}{S}
and whose log structure
M_{S_{0}^{[p]}(S)}

is a unique sub-log structure of
S

such that the isomorphism

M_{S}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}M_{S_{0}}/{\cal O}_{S_{0}}^{*}
induces
the following isomorphism

(1.0.2)

\displaystyle M_{S_{0}^{[p]}(S)}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Im}(F_{S_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}^{*}\colon F_{S_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}^{*}(M_{S_{0}^{[p]}})\longrightarrow M_{S_{0}})/{\cal O}_{S_{0}}^{*}.

(The structural morphism of
M_{S_{0}^{[p]}(S)}
is the composite morphism

M_{S_{0}^{[p]}(S)}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}M_{S}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S}
.)
We have an obvious morphism
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)\longrightarrow(S_{0}^{[p]}(S),{\cal I},\gamma)
of log PD-formal schemes.
For a fine log scheme
Y
over
S_{0}
with structural morphism
g\colon Y\longrightarrow S_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
,
let
(Y/S)_{\rm crys}
be the log crystalline topos of
Y/(S,{\cal I},\gamma)

defined in
[
Ka1
]
and let
{\cal O}_{Y/S}
be the structure sheaf of

(Y/S)_{\rm crys}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\rm zar}
be the Zariski topos of
\overset{\circ}{Y}
.
Let
u_{Y/S}\colon(Y/S)_{\rm crys}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\rm zar}
be the canonical projection.
Set
g_{Y/S}:=g\circ u_{Y/S}
.
Let
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
be the derived category of
bounded below filtered complexes of
g^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-modules
and let
D^{+}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
be the derived category of
bounded below complexes of
g^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-modules.
(See
[
NS
]
for the definition of the filtered derived category

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
(cf.
[
D1
]
,
[
Il1
]
))
In
[
N4
]
we have proved the following:

Theorem 1.1

(
[
N4
, Existence of the zarisikian
p
-adic
filtered Steenbrink complex]
).

Let
\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(m)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the crystalline orientation sheaf associated to
the set
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
for
m
. That is,

\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(m)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
is
the extension to
(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm crys}

of the direct sum of

\overset{m+1}{\bigwedge}{\mathbb{Z}}^{E}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{m}}}
’s
in the Zariski topos
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}_{\rm zar}
of
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}

for the subsets
E=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}},\ldots,\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{m}}\}
’s
of
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

with
\#E=m+1
.
Then there exists a filtered complex

(1.1.1)

\displaystyle(A_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

with a canonical isomorphism

(1.1.2)

\displaystyle\theta\wedge\colon Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A_{\rm zar}(X/S)

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

such that

(1.1.3)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}

\displaystyle a^{(2j+k)}_{*}(Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}})

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k)[-2j-k]

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
.
Here the Tate twist
(-j-k)
means the Tate twist
with respect to
the morphism
X\longrightarrow X\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}

over
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)\longrightarrow(S_{0}^{[p]}(S),{\cal I},\gamma)

induced by the absolute Frobenius endomorphism
F_{X}\colon X\longrightarrow X
of
X
.

As a corollary of this theorem, we obtain
the weight filtration
P
on
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})
:

(1.1.4)

\displaystyle P_{k+q}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

\displaystyle:={\rm Im}(R^{q}f_{X/S*}(P_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}(A_{\rm zar}(X/S)))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Im}(R^{q}f_{X/S*}(P_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})).

and the following spectral sequence

(1.1.5)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}:=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}

\displaystyle R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)/\overset{\circ}{S}}}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{h}f_{X/S}({\cal O}_{X/S})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Let
{\cal V}
be a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristics

(0,p)
with perfect residue field.
In
[
N4
]
we have proved that,
if
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme,
then (
1.1.5
) degenerates at
E_{2}
.

For a log smooth scheme
Y
over
S_{0}
,
let

(1.1.6)

N\colon Ru_{Y/S*}({\cal O}_{Y/S})\longrightarrow Ru_{Y/S*}({\cal O}_{Y/S})(-1).

be the monodromy operator defined in
[
HK
]
and
[
N4
]
.
In
[
N4
]
we have conjectured the following,
which is a generalization of the conjecture in
[
M
]
and a literal

p
-adic analogue of Kato’s conjecture in
[
Ka2
]
:

:

Conjecture 1.2

(
p
–adic monodromy-weight conjecture).

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a
p
-adic formal scheme and that

\overset{\circ}{X}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
is projective.
Let
q
be nonnegative integer.
Then the induced morphism

(1.2.1)

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+e}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P}_{q-e}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})(-e)\quad(q,e\in{\mathbb{N}})

by the monodromy operator (
1.1.6
)
is an isomorphism modulo torsion.

This conjecture has been solved in several special cases in
e. g.,
[
M
]
,
[
N1
]
,
[
N2
]
,
[
It
]
,
[
dS
]
,

[
LP
]
,
[
N4
]
and
[
BKV
]
.

In the rest of this introduction, we explain our main results, which includes
generalizations of (
1.1
) and (
1.2
).

Let
S_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
be a closed immersion defined by
a PD-ideal
{\cal I}
with PD-structure
\gamma
.
Let
(X,D)/S_{0}
be an SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD
with structural morphism
f\colon(X,D)\longrightarrow S_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
.
(We recall the definition of an SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD
in §
3
below.)
Let
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{2}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
be the
derived category of bifiltered complexes of
f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-modules.
(See
[
D2
]
and
[
N6
]
for the definition of

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{2}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
,)
Let
\{\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M}
be
the set of smooth components of
\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
.
For a positive integer
l
, let

(1.2.2)

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(l)}:=\coprod_{\{\{\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{l}\}~|\mu_{i}\in M,\mu_{i}\not=\mu_{j}(i\not=j)\}}\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu_{l}}

be an analogous scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
to (
1.0.1
).
Endow
\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(l)}
with the inverse image of the log structure of
X

and let
D^{(l)}
be the resulting log scheme over
S_{0}
.
Set
D^{(0)}:=X
.
Let
a^{(m,l)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(l)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
be the natural morphism.
Let

\varpi^{(m,l)}_{\rm crys}((\overset{\circ}{X}+\overset{\circ}{D})/\overset{\circ}{S})

be the crystalline orientation sheaf defined in §
4
below.
In this paper we prove the following:

Theorem 1.3
.

There exists a bifiltered complex

(1.3.1)

\displaystyle(A_{\rm zar}((X,D)/S),P^{D},P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{2}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

with a canonical isomorphism

(1.3.2)

\displaystyle\theta\wedge\colon Ru_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A_{\rm zar}((X,D)/S)

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

such that

(1.3.3)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm zar}((X,D)/S)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{k^{\prime}\leq k}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k^{\prime},0\}}a^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1,k-k^{\prime})}_{*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}

\displaystyle({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1,k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm crys}((\overset{\circ}{X}+\overset{\circ}{D})/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k)[-2j-k]

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
and

(1.3.4)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P^{D}}_{k}A_{\rm zar}((X,D)/S)=A_{\rm zar}(D^{(k)}/S)(-k)[-k].

As a corollary of this theorem, we obtain
the following weight filtration
P
on
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})
:

(1.3.5)

\displaystyle P_{k+q}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

\displaystyle:={\rm Im}(R^{q}f_{X/S*}(P_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}(A_{\rm zar}(X/S)))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Im}(R^{q}f_{X/S*}(P_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S}))

and the filtrration
P^{D}
on
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})
:

(1.3.5)

\displaystyle P^{D}_{k}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

\displaystyle:={\rm Im}(R^{q}f_{X/S*}(P^{D}_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}(A_{\rm zar}(X/S)))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Im}(R^{q}f_{X/S*}(P^{D}_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})).

We also obtain the following spectral sequences:

(1.1.6)

E_{1}^{-k,q+k}:=\bigoplus_{k^{\prime}\leq k}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k^{\prime},0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime},k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}+\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k)\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{Z}})

and

(1.1.7)

E_{1}^{-k,q+k}:=R^{q-k}f_{D^{(k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{D^{(k)}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-k)\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Here
\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})
is
the inverse image of

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})
by the
natural morphism

D\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{D}
forgetting the log structure of
D
.

In this paper we give the following conjecture:

Conjecture 1.4

(
Relative
p
–adic monodromy conjecture).

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a
p
–adic scheme and that

\overset{\circ}{X}
is projective over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then the relative monodromy filtration
M
on
R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})

with respect to the filtration
P^{D}
on
R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})
exists
and it is equal to
P
. That is, the monodromy operator

N\colon R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})(-1)

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})

induces the following isomorphism:

(1.4.1)

\displaystyle N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}_{q+k+e}^{P}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D}}R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\longrightarrow{\rm gr}_{q+k-e}^{P}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D}}R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})(-e)

for
e,k\in{\mathbb{N}}
is an isomorphism modulo torsion.

We prove that, if (
1.2
) is true for
D^{(k)}
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
then (
1.4
) is true. As a corollary of this result,
we obtain the following:

Theorem 1.5
.

The conjecture is true in the following cases
:

(1)
The relative dimension of
\overset{\circ}{X}
over
\overset{\circ}{S})
is less than or equal
2
.

(2)
For each connected component
S^{\prime}_{0}
of
S_{0}
, there exists an exact closed point
s\in S^{\prime}_{1}

such that the fiber
(X_{s},D_{s})/s
of
(X,D)/S_{0}
at
s
is the log special fiber
of a proper strict semistable family over a complete discrete valuation ring
of equal characteristic.

Notations.

(1) For a log scheme
X
,
\overset{\circ}{X}
denotes the underlying scheme of
X
.
For a morphism

\varphi\colon X\longrightarrow Y
,
\overset{\circ}{\varphi}
denotes
the underlying morphism

\overset{\circ}{X}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}
of
\varphi
.

(2) SNC(L)=simple normal crossing (log),
SNCD=simple normal crossing divisor.

(3) For a complex
(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})

of objects in an exact additive category
{\cal A}
,
we often denote
(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
only by

E^{\bullet}
as usual.

(4) For a complex
(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
in (3) and
for an integer
n
,
(E^{\bullet}\{n\},d^{\bullet}\{n\})

denotes the following complex:

\cdots\longrightarrow\underset{q-1}{E^{q-1+n}}\overset{d^{q-1+n}}{\longrightarrow}\underset{q}{E^{q+n}}\overset{d^{q+n}}{\longrightarrow}\underset{q+1}{E^{q+1+n}}\overset{d^{q+1+n}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

Here the numbers under the objects
above in
{\cal A}
mean the degrees.

(5) For a morphism

f\colon(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet}_{E})\longrightarrow(F^{\bullet},d^{\bullet}_{F})

of complexes,
let
{\rm MF}(f)
(resp. MC(f))
be the mapping fiber (resp. the mapping cone)
of
f
:
{\rm MF}(f):=E^{\bullet}\oplus F^{\bullet}[-1]

with boundary morphism “
(x,y)\longmapsto(d_{E}(x),-d_{F}(y)+f(x))
”
(resp.
{\rm MC}(f):=E^{\bullet}[1]\oplus F^{\bullet}

with boundary morphism “
(x,y)\longmapsto(-d_{E}(x),d_{F}(y)+f(x))
”).

(6) Let
({\cal T},{\cal A})
be a ringed topos.

(a)
C({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
{\rm C}^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

C^{\rm b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
): the category of
(resp. bounded below, bounded above,
bounded) complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules,

(b)
K({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
K^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

K^{b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
): the category of
(resp. bounded below, bounded above,
bounded) complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules modulo homotopy,

(b)
D({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
D^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

D^{b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
): the derived category of

K({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
K^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

K^{b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
).
For an object
E^{\bullet}
of

C({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
C^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

C^{b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
), we denote
simply by
E^{\bullet}

the corresponding object to
E^{\bullet}
in

D({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
D^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

D^{b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
).

(d) The additional notation
{\rm F}

to the categories above means “the filtered ”.
Here the filtration is
an increasing filtration indexed by

{\mathbb{Z}}
.
For example,

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal T},{\cal A})
is
the category
of bounded below filtered complexes
modulo filtered homotopy.

(e)
{\rm DF}^{2}({\cal T},{\cal A})

(resp.
{\rm D}^{\pm}{\rm F}^{2}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{2}({\cal T},{\cal A})
):
the derived category of (resp. bounded below, bounded above, bounded)
bifiltered complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules defined in
[
N6
]
.

2
SNCL schemes

In this section we recall the definition of an SNCL(=simple normal crossing log) scheme
defined in
[
N4
]
and
[
N5
]
.

Let
S
be a log formal scheme with ideal of definition
{\cal J}

(
{\cal J}
may be the zero ideal
(0)
)
such that there exists an open covering

S=\bigcup_{i\in I}S_{i}
such that
M_{S_{i}}

is the association of the log structure
is
{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S_{i}}^{*}
with a morphism

{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S_{i}}^{*}\owns(n,a)\longmapsto 0^{n}a\in{\cal O}_{S_{i}}
,
where
0^{0}:=1\in{\cal O}_{S_{i}}
.
In
[
N4
]
we have called
S
a formal family of log points.
When
(\overset{\circ}{S},{\cal I},\gamma)
is
a formal PD-scheme with quasi-coherent PD-ideal sheaf
and PD-structure,
we call
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
a formal PD-family of log points.

Let
S=\bigcup_{i\in I}S_{i}
be an open covering
of
S
such that

M_{S_{i}}/{\cal O}^{*}_{S_{i}}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}
.
Take a local section

t_{i}\in\Gamma(S_{i},M_{S})

(i\in I)
such that
the image of
t_{i}
in
\Gamma(S_{i},M_{S}/{\cal O}^{*}_{S})
is a generator.
Set
S_{ij}:=S_{i}\cap S_{j}
.
Then there exists a unique section

u_{ji}\in\Gamma(S_{ij},{\cal O}^{*}_{S})
such that

t_{j}|_{S_{ij}}=u_{ji}(t_{i}|_{S_{ij}})

in
\Gamma(S_{ij},M_{S})
.
Denote
\underline{\rm Spf}_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}}({\cal O}_{S_{i}}\{\tau_{i}]\}
by

{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}}
, where
\tau_{i}
is a variable.
Endow

{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}}
with the log structure

({\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto\tau_{i}\in{\cal O}_{S_{i}}\{\tau_{i}\})^{a})
.
Denote the resulting log scheme by
\overline{S}_{i}
.
Then, by patching
\overline{S}_{i}
and
\overline{S}_{j}
along

\overline{S}_{ij}:=\overline{S}_{i}\cap\overline{S}_{j}
by the equation

\tau_{j}|_{\overline{S}_{ij}}=u_{ji}\tau_{i}|_{\overline{S}_{ij}}
,
we have a log formal scheme
\overline{S}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\overline{S}_{i}
.
The ideal sheaves
\tau_{i}{\cal O}_{\overline{S}_{i}}
’s (
i\in I
)
patch together and we denote by

{\cal I}_{\overline{S}}

the resulting ideal sheaf of

{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}
.
The isomorphism class of the log scheme
\overline{S}

is independent of the choice of the system of
generators
\tau_{i}
’s.
We see that the isomorphism class
of the log scheme
\overline{S}
and the ideal sheaf

{\cal I}_{\overline{S}}

are also independent of
the choice of the open covering
S=\bigcup_{i\in I}S_{i}
.
The natural morphism
\overline{S}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
is formally log smooth
by the criterion of the log smoothness (
[
Ka1
, (3.5)]
).
For a log scheme
Y
over
\overline{S}
,
we denote

{\cal I}_{\overline{S}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}}{\cal O}_{Y}

by
{\cal I}_{Y}
by abuse of notation.

By killing
{\cal I}_{\overline{S}}
,
we have a natural exact closed immersion

S\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{S}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.

Let
B
be a scheme.
For two nonnegative integers
a
and
d
such that

a\leq d
,
consider the following scheme

\overset{\circ}{\mathbb{A}}_{B}(a,d):=\underline{{\rm Spf}}_{B}({\cal O}_{B}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}]/(\prod_{i=0}^{a}x_{i})).

Definition 2.1

(
[
N4
, (1.1.9)]
).

Let
Z
be a scheme over
B

with structural morphism
g\colon Z\longrightarrow B
.
We call
Z
an

SNC
(
=simple normal crossing
)
scheme
over

B
if
Z
is a union of smooth schemes

\{Z_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
over
B
(
\Lambda
is a set)
and if, for any point of
z\in Z
,
there exist an open neighborhood
V
of

z
and an open neighborhood
W
of

g(z)
such that
there exists an étale morphism

V\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\mathbb{A}}_{W}(a,d)

such that

(2.1.1)

\{Z_{\lambda}|_{V}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}=\{\{x_{i}=0\}\}_{i=0}^{a},

where
a
and
d
are nonnegative integers
such that
a\leq d
,
which depend on
zariskian local neighborhoods in
Z
.
Here
\{Z_{\lambda}|_{V}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
means the set of

Z_{\lambda}|_{V}
’s such that
Z_{\lambda}|_{V}\not=\emptyset
by abuse of notation.
We call the set
\{Z_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

a
decomposition of
Z
by smooth components of

Z
over
B
.
We call
Z_{\lambda}
a

smooth component
of
Z
over
B
.

Set
\Delta:=\{Z_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.
For an open subscheme
V
of
Z
,
set
\Delta_{V}:=\{Z_{\lambda}|_{V}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.
For a nonnegative integer
m
and a subset

\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{0},\cdots,\lambda_{m}\}

(\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}~{\rm if}~i\not=j,\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda)
of
\Lambda
,
set

(2.1.2)

Z_{\underline{\lambda}}:=Z_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap Z_{\lambda_{m}}.

Set

(2.1.3)

Z^{(m)}:=\underset{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}{\coprod}Z_{\underline{\lambda}}

for
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and

Z_{\phi}=Z
(
\phi
is the empty set) and
Z^{(-1)}=Z
.
We also set
Z^{(m)}=\emptyset
for
m\leq-2
.
Note that, for an element
\lambda
of
\Lambda
,

Z_{\{\lambda\}}=Z_{\lambda}
; we have to use both notations

Z_{\{\lambda\}}
and
Z_{\lambda}
.
In
[
N4
, (1.1.12)]
we have proved that
Z^{(m)}

is independent of the choice of
\Delta
.
We have the natural morphism

Z^{(m)}\longrightarrow Z
.

As in
[
D1
, (3.1.4)]
and
[
NS
, (2.2.18)]
,
we have an orientation sheaf
\varpi^{(m)}_{\rm zar}(Z/B)

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})
in
Z^{(m)}_{\rm zar}

associated to the set
\Delta
.
If
B
is a closed subscheme of
B^{\prime}
defined by
a quasi-coherent nil-ideal sheaf
{\cal J}

which has a PD-structure
\delta
,
then
\varpi^{(m)}_{\rm zar}(Z/B)

extends to an abelian sheaf

\varpi^{(m)}_{\rm crys}(Z/B^{\prime})
in

(Z^{(m)}/(B^{\prime},{\cal J},\delta))_{\rm crys}
.

Assume that
S
is a scheme
and that
M_{S}
is the free log structure of rank 1
for the time being.
We fix an isomorphism

\displaystyle(M_{S},\alpha_{S})\simeq({\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S})

globally on
S
.
Let
M_{S}(a,d)
be the log structure on

{\mathbb{A}}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}(a,d)
associated to the following morphism

(2.1.4)

{\mathbb{N}}^{{\oplus}(a+1)}\owns(0,\ldots,0,\overset{i}{1},0,\ldots,0)\longmapsto x_{i-1}\in{\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}]/(\prod_{i=0}^{a}x_{i}).

Let
{\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,d)
be the resulting log scheme over
S
.
The diagonal morphism
{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{N}}^{{\oplus}(a+1)}

induces a morphism
{\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,d)\longrightarrow S
of log schemes.

Definition 2.2
.

Let
S
be a family of log points
(we do not assume that
M_{S}
is free).
Let
f\colon X(=(\overset{\circ}{X},M_{X}))\longrightarrow S

be a morphism of log schemes
such that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is
an SNC scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}

with a decomposition

\Delta:=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

of
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}
by its smooth components.
We call
f
(or
X/S
) an

SNCL
(
=simple normal crossing log
)
scheme
if,
for any point of
x\in\overset{\circ}{X}
,
there exist an open neighborhood
\overset{\circ}{V}
of

x
and an open neighborhood
\overset{\circ}{W}
of

\overset{\circ}{f}(x)
such that
M_{W}
is
the free hollow log structure of rank
1

and such that
f|_{V}
factors through
a solid and étale morphism

V{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{A}}_{W}(a,d)

such that
\Delta_{\overset{\circ}{V}}=\{x_{i}=0\}_{i=0}^{a}
in
\overset{\circ}{V}
.
(Similarly we can give the definition of
a formal SNCL scheme over
S

in the case where
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a formal scheme.)

Let
X
be an SNCL scheme over
S

with a decomposition

\Delta:=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

of
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}

by its smooth components.
We set
X_{\emptyset}:=X
for convenience of notation.
Let

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}

be
the natural morphism.

Assume that
M_{S}\simeq({\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto 0\in{\cal O}_{S})^{a}
.
Then
\overline{S}=({\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{S}},({\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto t\in{\cal O}_{S}[t])^{a})
.
Set

\overset{\circ}{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d):=\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d},t]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a}-t)).

Then we have a natural structural morphism

\overset{\circ}{{\mathbb{A}}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}
.
Let
\overline{M}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)

be the log structure associated to
a morphism

{\mathbb{N}}^{a+1}\owns e_{i}=(0,\ldots,0,\overset{i}{1},0,\ldots,0)\longmapsto x_{i-1}\in{\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d},t]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a}-t).

Set

{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d):=(\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d},t]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a}-t)),\overline{M}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)).

Then we have the following natural morphism

(2.2.1)

{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)\longrightarrow\overline{S}.

By killing “
t
”, we have the following natural exact closed immersion

(2.2.2)

{\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,d)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)

of fs(=fine and saturated) log schemes
over
S\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{S}

if the log structure of
S
is
the free hollow log structure of rank 1.

Definition 2.3

(
A special case of
[
N4
, (1.1.16)]
).

Let
\overline{f}\colon\overline{X}\longrightarrow\overline{S}

be a morphism of log schemes (on the Zariski sites).
Set
X:=\overline{X}\times_{\overline{S}}S
.
We call
\overline{f}
(or
\overline{X}/\overline{S}
)
a
strict semistable log scheme
over
\overline{S}

if
\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}
is a smooth scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}
,
if
\overset{\circ}{X}
is a relative SNCD on

\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}/\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}

(with some decomposition

\Delta:=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of

\overset{\circ}{X}
by smooth components of

\overset{\circ}{X}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
)
and if, for any point of
x\in\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}
,
there exist an open neighborhood
\overset{\circ}{\overline{V}}
of

x
and an open neighborhood

\overset{\circ}{\overline{W}}\simeq\underline{\rm Spec}_{W}({\cal O}_{W}[t])

(where
W:=\overline{W}\times_{\overline{S}}S
) of

\overset{\circ}{f}(x)
such that

M_{\overline{W}}\simeq({\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto t\in{\cal O}_{W}[t])^{a}
and such that

\overline{f}|_{\overline{V}}
factors through
a solid and étale morphism

\overline{V}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{W}}(a,d)

such that

\Delta_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{V}}}=\{x_{i}=0\}_{i=0}^{a}
in
\overset{\circ}{\overline{V}}
.
(Similarly we can give the definition of
a strict semistable log formal scheme over
\overline{S}
.)

3
SNCL schemes with relative SNCD’s

In this section we recall the definition of an SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD
in
[
NY
]
and we give elementary result on it.
First let us recall the following definition.

Definition 3.1

(
[
NY
, (6.1)]
).

(1) Let
S
be a family of log points and let
X/S
be an SNCL scheme.
Let
{\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,d-b)\times_{S}{\mathbb{A}}^{b}_{S}={\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,d)

(0\leq a\leq d-b,b\leq d)

be a log scheme whose
underlying scheme is
\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d-b},y_{1},\ldots,y_{b}]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a}))
and whose
log structure is the association
of the following morphism

{\mathbb{N}}^{\oplus a+1}\owns e_{i}\longmapsto x_{i-1}\in{\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d-b},y_{1},\ldots,y_{b}]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a}).

Let
{\rm Div}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\geq 0}

be the set of effective Cartier divisors on
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{D}
be an effective Cartier divisor on
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{D}
with the inverse image of the log structure of
X
and
let
D
be the resulting log scheme.
We call
D
a
relative simple normal crossing divisor

(=:
relative SNCD
)
on
X/S
if there exists a family

\Delta:=\{\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M}
of
non-zero effective Cartier divisors on
X/S

of locally finite intersection which are
SNC(=simple normal crossing) schemes over
S
such that

(3.1.1)

\overset{\circ}{D}=\sum_{\mu\in M}\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}\quad\text{in}\quad{\rm Div}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\geq 0}

and,
for any point
z
of
\overset{\circ}{D}
, there exist
a Zariski open neighborhood
\overset{\circ}{V}
of
z
in
\overset{\circ}{X}
and
the following cartesian diagram

(3.1.2)

\begin{CD}D|_{V}@>{}>{}>(y_{1}\cdots y_{b}=0)\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
V@>{g}>{}>{\mathbb{A}}_{S^{\prime}}(a,d-e)\times_{S^{\prime}}{\mathbb{A}}^{e}_{S^{\prime}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S^{\prime}=S^{\prime}\end{CD}

for some nonnegative integers
a
,
b
,
d
and
e

such that
a\leq d-e
and
b\leq e\leq d
.
Here
S^{\prime}
is an open log subscheme of
S

whose log structure is associated to
the morphism
{\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto 0\in{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}
,

(y_{1}\cdots y_{b}=0)
is an exact closed log subscheme of

{\mathbb{A}}_{S^{\prime}}(a,d-e)\times_{T}{\mathbb{A}}^{e}_{S^{\prime}}
defined by an ideal sheaf
(y_{1}\cdots y_{b})
,

g
is solidly log étale and
{\mathbb{A}}_{S^{\prime}}(a,d-e)\times_{S^{\prime}}{\mathbb{A}}^{e}_{S^{\prime}}
is obtained by
the diagonal embedding
{\mathbb{N}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{N}}^{\oplus a+1}
.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}
with the inverse image of
the log structure of
X
and let
D_{\mu}
be the resulting log scheme.
We call
D_{\mu}
an
SNCL component
of
D
and
the equality (
3.1.1
) a
decomposition
of
D

by SNCL components of
D
.

Before
[
NY
, (6.2)]
we have constructed a log structure
M(D)

in the zariski topos
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm zar}
as in
[
NS
, p. 61]
.
and in
[
NY
, (6.2)]
we have proved the following:

Proposition 3.2
.

Let the notations be as above.
Let
z
be a point of
D
and let

V
be an open neighborhood of
z
in
X

in the diagram
(
3.1.2
)
.
Assume that
z\in\bigcap_{i=1}^{b}\{y_{i}=0\}
.
If
V
is small, then the log structure

M(D)|_{V}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{V}
is
isomorphic to
{\cal O}_{V}^{*}y_{1}^{{\mathbb{N}}}\cdots y_{b}^{{\mathbb{N}}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{V}
.
Consequently
M(D)|_{V}
is associated to
the homomorphism

{\mathbb{N}}^{b}_{V}\owns e_{i}\longmapsto y_{i}\in M(D)|_{V}

(1\leq i\leq b)
of sheaves of monoids on
V
,
where
\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{b}
is the canonical
basis of
{\mathbb{N}}^{b}
.
In particular,
M(D)
is fs.

Set

\displaystyle(X,D):=(X,M_{X}\oplus_{{\cal O}^{*}_{X}}M(D)\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{X}).

Then
(X,D)/S
is log smooth, integral and saturated.
This is nothing but the fiber product of
(X,M_{X})
and

(\overset{\circ}{X},M(D))
over
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
As in the classical case (e. g.,
[
D1
]
),
we can consider the log de Rham complex

\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/S}(\log D)
with logarithmic poles along
D
.
It is clear that
the complex
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/S}(\log D)
is equal to
the log de Rham complex
\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D)/S}
.

For
\underline{\mu}:=\{\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\ldots\mu_{k}\}

(\mu_{i}\not=\mu_{j}~{\rm if}~i\not=j)
,
set

D_{\underline{\mu}}:=D_{\mu_{1}}\cap D_{\mu_{2}}\cap\cdots\cap D_{\mu_{k}}

for a positive integer
k
and set

D^{(k)}=\begin{cases}\quad X&(k=0),\\
\underset{\#\underline{\mu}=k}{\coprod}D_{\underline{\mu}}&(k\geq 1)\end{cases}

for a nonnegative integer
k
.
We see that

D^{(k)}
is independent of the choice of
the decomposition of
D
by SNCL components of
D
.

Set

{\mathbb{A}}_{S^{\prime}}(a,b,d,e):=({\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S^{\prime}}}(a,d-e)\times_{\overline{S}}{\mathbb{A}}^{e}_{\overline{S^{\prime}}},(y_{1}\cdots y_{b}=0))

for
a\leq d-e
and
b\leq e\leq d
.
Let
{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S^{\prime}}}(a,b,d,e)
be a log scheme
whose underlying scheme is

\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overline{S^{\prime}}}({\cal O}_{S}[t][x_{0},\ldots,x_{d-b},y_{1},\ldots,y_{e}]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a}-t))

and whose log structure is the association
of the following morphism

{\mathbb{N}}^{\oplus a+b+1}={\mathbb{N}}^{\oplus a+1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\oplus b}\in{\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d-b},y_{1},\ldots,y_{e}]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a}-t)

defined by

\owns e_{i}\longmapsto x_{i-1}
for
1\leq i\leq d-b
and
\owns e_{i}\longmapsto y_{i-(a+1)}
for
a+1<i\leq a+b+1
.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
S
be a family of log points and let
\overline{X}/\overline{S}
be a strictly semistable scheme.
Let
\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}
be an effective Cartier divisor on
\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}/\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}
.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}
with the inverse image of the log structure of
\overline{X}
and
let
\overline{D}
be the resulting log scheme.
We call
\overline{D}
a
relative simple normal crossing divisor

(=:
relative SNCD
)
on
\overline{X}/\overline{S}
if there exists a family

\overline{\Delta}:=\{\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of
non-zero effective Cartier divisors on
X/S

of locally finite intersection which are
strictly semistable schemes over
S
such that

(3.3.1)

\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}=\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}_{\lambda}\quad\text{in}\quad{\rm Div}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}/\overline{S})_{\geq 0}

and,
for any point
z
of
\overset{\circ}{D}
, there exist
a Zariski open neighborhood
\overset{\circ}{V}
of
z
in
\overset{\circ}{X}
and
the following cartesian diagram

(3.3.2)

\begin{CD}\overline{D}|_{V}@>{}>{}>(y_{1}\cdots y_{b}=0)\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
V@>{g}>{}>{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}{}^{\prime}}(a,d-e)\times_{T}{\mathbb{A}}^{e}_{S^{\prime}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\overline{S}{}^{\prime}=\overline{S}{}^{\prime}\end{CD}

for some nonnegaitive integers
a
,
b
,
d
and
e

such that
0\leq a\leq d-e
and
b\leq e\leq d
.
Here
\overline{S}{}^{\prime}
is an open log subscheme of
\overline{S}

whose log structure is associated to
the morphism
{\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto t\in{\cal O}_{\overline{S}{}^{\prime}}
,

(y_{1}\cdots y_{b}=0)
is an exact closed log subscheme of

{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}{}^{\prime}}(a,d-e)\times_{T}{\mathbb{A}}^{e}_{S^{\prime}}
defined by
an ideal sheaf
(y_{1}\cdots y_{b})
,

g
is solidly log étale and
{\mathbb{A}}_{S^{\prime}}(a,d-e)\times_{S^{\prime}}{\mathbb{A}}^{e}_{S^{\prime}}
is obtained by
the diagonal embedding
{\mathbb{N}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{N}}^{\oplus a+1}
.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}_{\lambda}
with the inverse image of
the log structure of
X
and let
\overline{D}_{\lambda}
be the resulting log scheme.
We call
\overline{D}_{\lambda}
an
strictly semistable component
of
\overline{D}
and
the equality (
3.1.1
) a
decomposition
of
\overline{D}

by strictly semistable components of
\overline{D}
.

Lemma 3.4

(
A special case of
[
N4
, (1.1.6)]
).

Let
S
be a family of log points. Then the following hold
:

(1)
Let
Y\longrightarrow S
be a log smooth scheme
which has a global chart
{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow P
.
Then, Zariski locally on
Y
,
there exists a log smooth scheme

\overline{Y}
over
\overline{S}
fitting into
the following cartesian diagram

(3.4.1)

\begin{CD}Y@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{Y}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S\times_{{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{N}}])}{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[P])@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S}\times_{{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{N}}])}{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[P])\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S},\end{CD}

where the vertical morphism

\overline{Y}\longrightarrow\overline{S}\times_{{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{N}}])}{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[P])

is solid and étale.

(2)
Let
S
be a family of log points.
Let
X
be an SNCL scheme over
S
with a relative SNCD

D
on
X/S
.
Zariski locally on
X
, there exists a strictly semistable log scheme

\overline{X}
over
\overline{S}
with a relative SNCD
\overline{D}
fitting into
the following cartesian diagram for
0\leq a\leq d-e
and
b\leq e\leq d
:

(3.4.2)

\begin{CD}(X,D)@>{\subset}>{}>(\overline{X},\overline{D})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,b,d,e)@>{\subset}>{}>{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,b,d,e)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S},\end{CD}

where the vertical morphism

(\overline{X},\overline{D})\longrightarrow{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,b,d,e)
is solid and étale.

We also recall the following (
[
NS
, (2.1.5)]
) describing
the local structure of an exact closed immersion,
which will be used in this section and later sections:

Proposition 3.5

(
[
NS
, (2.1.5)]
).

Let
T_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T
be a closed immersion of fine log schemes.
Let
Y

(
resp.
{\cal Q})
be a log smooth scheme over
T_{0}

(
resp.
T)
, which can be considered as a log scheme over
T
.
Let
\iota\colon Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}

be an exact closed immersion over
T
.
Let
y
be a point of
\overset{\circ}{Y}
and
assume that there exists a chart
(Q\longrightarrow M_{T},P\longrightarrow M_{Y},Q\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}P)
of
Y\longrightarrow T_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T

on a neighborhood of
y
such that

\rho
is injective,
{\rm Coker}(\rho^{\rm gp})
is torsion free
and the natural homomorphism
{\cal O}_{Y,y}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(P^{{\rm gp}}/Q^{{\rm gp}})\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{Y/T_{0},y}
is an isomorphism.
Then, on a neighborhood of
y
,
there exist a nonnegative integer
c

and the following cartesian diagram

(3.5.1)

{\small{\begin{CD}Y@>{}>{}>{\cal Q}^{\prime}@>{}>{}>{\cal Q}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(T_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[Q]}{\mathbb{Z}}[P],P^{a})@>{\subset}>{}>(T\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[Q]}{\mathbb{Z}}[P],P^{a})@>{\subset}>{}>(T\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[Q]}{\mathbb{Z}}[P],P^{a})\times_{T}{\mathbb{A}}^{c}_{T},\end{CD}}}

where the vertical morphisms are solid and étale and
the lower second horizontal morphism is the base change of
the zero section
T\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{A}}^{c}_{T}

and
{\cal Q}^{\prime}:={\cal Q}\times_{{\mathbb{A}}^{c}_{T}}T
.

Let
S_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S

be a nil-immersion of families of log points.
Let
X/S_{0}
be an SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD
D
on
X/S_{0}
.
Let

(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
be
an immersion into a log smooth scheme over
S
.
Let

(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}
be also
an immersion into a log smooth scheme over
\overline{S}
.
(We do not assume that there exists an immersion

{\cal P}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}
).

Proposition 3.6
.

Assume that

(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}

(
resp.
(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P})

has a global chart

P\longrightarrow Q

(
resp.
\overline{P}\longrightarrow Q)
.
Let
P^{\rm ex}

(
resp.
\overline{P}{}^{\rm ex})

be the inverse image of

Q
by the morphism
P^{\rm gp}\longrightarrow Q^{\rm gp}

(
resp.
\overline{P}{}^{\rm gp}\longrightarrow Q^{\rm gp})
.
Set
{\cal P}^{\rm prex}:={\cal P}\times_{{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[P])}{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[P^{\rm ex}])

(
resp.
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm prex}:=\overline{\cal P}\times_{{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[\overline{P}])}{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[\overline{P}{}^{\rm ex}]))
.
Then, locally on
X
, there exists an open neighborhood

{\cal P}^{\rm prex}{}^{\prime}

(
resp.
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm prex}{}^{\prime})

of
{\cal P}^{\rm prex}

(
resp.
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm prex})

fitting into
the following cartesian diagram
for some
0\leq a\leq d-e
and
b\leq e\leq d\leq d^{\prime}:

(3.6.1)

\begin{CD}(X,D)@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}^{\rm prex}{}^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathbb{A}}_{S_{0}}(a,b,d,e)@>{\subset}>{}>{\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,b,d^{\prime},e)\\
\end{CD}

(
resp.

(3.6.2)

\begin{CD}X@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm prex}{}^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathbb{A}}_{S_{0}}(a,b,d,e)@>{\subset}>{}>{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,b,d^{\prime},e),\end{CD}

where the vertical morphisms are solid and étale.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of
[
N4
, (1.1.40)]
by using (
3.5
).
∎

Proposition 3.7

(
cf.
[
N4
, (1.1.41)]
).

(1)
Let
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
be the exactification of
the immersion
(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
.
Then
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
is a formal SNCL scheme over
S
with
a unique relative SNCD
{\cal D}
on
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S
such that

{\cal D}\times_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}X=D
.

(2)
Let
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

be the exactification of
the immersion
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}
.
Then
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

is a formal strict semistable family over
\overline{S};

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}
with a unique relative SNCD

\overline{\cal D}
on
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overline{S}
such that

\overline{\cal D}\times_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}X=D
.

Proof.

Because the proof of (1) is the same as that of that of (2), we give the proof of (2).
Since the immersion
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
is exact,
the natural morphism

(M_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}/{\cal O}^{*}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}})_{x}\longrightarrow(M_{(X,D)}/{\cal O}^{*}_{X})_{x}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}^{\oplus a+1}\oplus{\mathbb{N}}^{\oplus b}

is an isomorphism.
The local coordinates of
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
corresponding to

{\mathbb{N}}^{\oplus a+1}
tells us that
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
is a formal
strict semistable scheme over
\overline{S}
;
the local coordinates of
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
corresponding to

{\mathbb{N}}^{\oplus b}
tells us that
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
has a
a relative SNCD

\overline{\cal D}
on
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overline{S}
such that

\overline{\cal D}\times_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}X=D
.
The uniqueness of
{\cal D}
is obvious since
the underlying topological space of

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}
is equal to that of
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
∎

Let
(X,D)
be an SNCL scheme over
S
with a relative SNCD.
Let
S
be a family of log points. Let
M_{S}=(M_{S},\alpha_{S})
be the log structure of
S
.
In
[
N4
]
we have defined a log PD-enlargement

((T,{\cal J},\delta),z)
of
S
as follows (cf.
[
O2
]
):

(T,{\cal J},\delta)
is a fine log PD-scheme such that
{\cal J}
is quasi-coherent and

z\colon T_{0}\longrightarrow S
is a morphism of fine log schemes,
where
T_{0}:=T\mod{\cal J}
.
When we are given a morphism
S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
of families of log points,
we can define a morphism of log PD-enlargements over
the morphism
S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
in an obvious way.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
with the inverse image of the log structure of
S
.
We denote the resulting log scheme by
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
It is easy to see that the natural morphism
z^{*}(M_{S})\longrightarrow M_{T_{0}}
is injective
(
[
N4
, (1.1.4)]
). Hence we can consider
z^{*}(M_{S})
is the sub log structure of
M_{T_{0}}
.
Let
M
be the sub log structure of the log structure
(M_{T},\alpha_{T})
of
T
such that
the natural morphism
M_{T}\longrightarrow M_{T_{0}}
induces an isomorphism

M/{\cal O}_{T}^{*}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}z^{*}(M_{S})/{\cal O}_{T_{0}}^{*}
.
Let
S(T)
be the log scheme
(\overset{\circ}{T},(M,\alpha_{T}|_{M}))
.
Since
M/{\cal O}_{T}^{*}
is constant, we can consider the hollowing out

S(T)^{\natural}
of
S(T)
(
[
O2
, Remark 7]
);
the log scheme
S(T)^{\natural}
is a family of log points.
Set
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}:=X\times_{S}S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}=X\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}

(we can consider
X
as a fine log scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}
).
By abuse of notation, we denote by the same symbol
f

the structural morphism

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
Let

a^{(l,m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(l)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

be the base change morphisms of
a^{(l,m)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(l)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(m)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
.

Let
(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})^{\prime}=\coprod_{i\in I}(X,D)_{i}
be the disjoint union
of an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
over
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

(
(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{i}
is a log open subscheme of
(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})
).
Assume that
f((X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{i}^{\circ})
is contained in
an affine open subscheme of

\overset{\circ}{T}=(S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})^{\circ}

such that the restriction of
M_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}

to this open subscheme is free of rank
1
.
Assume also that there exists a solid and log étale morphism

(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{i}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{A}}_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}(a,b,d,e)
.
Then, replacing
(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{i}
by a small log open subscheme of

(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})
,
we can assume that there exists a log smooth scheme

\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{i}/\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
fitting into the following commutative diagram

(3.7.1)

\begin{CD}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{i}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{i}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathbb{A}}_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}(a,b,d,e)@>{\subset}>{}>{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}(a,b,d^{\prime},e)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S(T)^{\natural}},\end{CD}

where
d\leq d^{\prime}
and the morphism

\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{i}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}(a,b,d,e)

is solid and étale ((
3.4
)).
Set
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}:=\coprod_{i\in I}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{i}
.
Set also

(3.7.2)

(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},n},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},n}):={\rm cosk}_{0}^{(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})}((X,D)^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{n}\quad(n\in{\mathbb{N}})

and

(3.7.3)

\overline{\cal P}_{n}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}(\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime})_{n}\quad(n\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Then we have a simplicial SNCL scheme
(X,D)_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}

with a relative SNCD
and an immersion

(3.7.4)

(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}

into a log smooth simplicial log scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Thus we have obtained the following:

Proposition 3.8
.

The following hold
:

(1)
There exist a Čech diagram

(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})
of
(X,D)
and an immersion

(3.8.1)

\begin{CD}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}\end{CD}

into a log smooth simplicial log scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

(2)
There exists a Čech diagram

(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})
of
(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})

and an immersion

(3.8.2)

\begin{CD}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{\subset}>{}>S(T)^{\natural}\end{CD}

into a log smooth simplicial log scheme over
S(T)^{\natural}
.

Corollary 3.9
.

Let the notations be as above.
Then there exist an exact immersion

(3.9.1)

\begin{CD}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>(\overline{\cal X}_{\bullet},\overline{\cal D}_{\bullet})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}\end{CD}

into a simplicial strict semistable log formal scheme with a relative SNCD over

\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
and an exact immersion

(3.9.2)

\begin{CD}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>({\cal X}_{\bullet},{\cal D}_{\bullet})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{\subset}>{}>S(T)^{\natural}\end{CD}

into a simplicial formal SNCL scheme over
S(T)^{\natural}
.

Proof.

This follows from (
3.7
) and (
3.8
).
∎

We conclude this section by recalling
the “mapping degree function” defined in
[
N4
]
.

Let
v\colon S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
be a morphism of families of log points.
Let
y
be a point of
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let
h\colon{\mathbb{N}}=M_{S^{\prime},v(x)}/{\cal O}^{*}_{S^{\prime},v(x)}\longrightarrow M_{S,y}/{\cal O}^{*}_{S,y}={\mathbb{N}}

be the induced morphism.
Let
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
be the image of
1\in{\mathbb{N}}
by
h
.

Definition 3.10

(
A special case of
[
N4
, (1.1.42)]
).

We call
\deg(v)_{x}:=d

the
(
mapping
)
degree
of
v
at
x
.
We call
\deg(v)\colon\overset{\circ}{S}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}

the
(
mapping
)
degree function
of
v
.

4
Preweight filtrations

Let
Y
be a fine log (formal) scheme
over a fine log (formal) scheme
U

with structural morphism
g\colon Y\longrightarrow U
.
Let
(N_{Y},\alpha|_{N_{Y}})
be a sub log structure of the log structure of
(M_{Y},\alpha)
.
Set
Y_{N_{Y}}:=(\overset{\circ}{Y},N_{Y})
.
We define the
pre-weight filtration

P^{M_{Y}\setminus N_{Y}}
on the sheaf
{\Omega}^{i}_{Y/U}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

of log differential forms on
\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\rm zar}
with respect to

M_{Y}\setminus N_{Y}
as follows:

(4.0.1)

P^{M_{Y}\setminus N_{Y}}_{k}{\Omega}^{i}_{Y/U}=\begin{cases}0&(k<0),\\
{\rm Im}({\Omega}^{k}_{Y/U}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{Y}}\Omega^{i-k}_{Y_{N}/U}\longrightarrow{\Omega}^{i}_{Y/U})&(0\leq k\leq i),\\
{\Omega}^{i}_{Y/U}&(k>i).\end{cases}

Let
g\colon Y\longrightarrow Z
be a morphism of fine log (formal) schemes over
U
.
Let
N_{Y}
and
N_{Z}
be sub log structures of
Y
and
Z
, respectively.
Assume that
g
induces a morphism

Y_{N_{Y}}:=(\overset{\circ}{Y},N_{Y})\longrightarrow Z_{N_{Z}}:=(\overset{\circ}{Z},N_{Z})
over
U
.
For a flat
{\cal O}_{Y}
-module
{\cal E}

and a flat
{\cal O}_{Z}
-module
{\cal F}
with a morphism

h\colon{\cal F}\longrightarrow g_{*}({\cal E})
of
{\cal O}_{Z}
-modules,
we have the following morphism of filtered complexes:

(4.0.2)

h\colon({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{Z}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{U}},P^{M_{Z}\setminus N_{Z}})\longrightarrow g_{*}(({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{Y}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{U}},P^{M_{Y}\setminus N_{Y}})).

The following is a slight generalization of
[
N4
, (1.3.4)]
:

Proposition 4.1

(
cf.
[
N4
, (1.3.4)]
).

Assume that
U
has a PD-structure
({\cal J},\delta)
.
Let
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}
be an immersion into a log smooth scheme over

(U,{\cal J},\delta)
.
Let
{\mathfrak{E}}
be the log PD-envelope of the immersion

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}
over
(U,{\cal J},\delta)
.
Let
M_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}
be the log structure of
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

and let
N_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}
be the sub log structure of
M_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}
.
Then the natural morphism

(4.1.1)

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}P^{M_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}\setminus N_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/U}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/U}\quad(i,k\in{\mathbb{Z}})

is injective.

Proof.

(The proof of this propositon is the same
as that of
[
NS
, (2.2.17) (1)]
.)
The question is local on
Y
;
we may assume the existence of
the commutative diagram (
3.5.1
).
Let
P^{\rm ex}
be the inverse image of

Q
by the morphism
P^{\rm gp}\longrightarrow Q^{\rm gp}
.
Then the natural morphism
P^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow Q
is surjective.
We have a fine log formal
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-scheme

{\cal Q}^{{\rm prex}}:={\cal Q}\times_{{\rm Spf}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\{P\})}{\rm Spf}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\{P^{\rm ex}\})

over
{\cal Q}
with a morphism
Y\longrightarrow{\cal Q}^{{\rm prex}}
.
Let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{c}
be the coordinates of

{\mathbb{A}}^{c}_{T}
in (
3.5.1
).
Set
{\cal K}:=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{c}){\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}

and
{\cal Q}^{\prime}:=\underline{\rm Spec}^{\log}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}({\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}/{\cal K})
.
Then
{\cal Q}^{\prime}
is a log smooth lift of
Y
over
T:=U
.
Let
M_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}}
and
N_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}}
be the inverse image of

M_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}
and
N_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}
, respectively.
Let
e
be a positive integer.
Since
{\cal Q}^{\prime}
is log smooth over
T
,
there exists a section of the surjection

{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}/{\cal K}^{e}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}/{\cal K}={\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}}
.
Set
{\cal K}_{0}:=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{c})
in

{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{c}]
.
Then, as in
[
BO1
, 3.32 Proposition]
, we have a morphism

{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{c}]\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}/{\cal K}^{e}

such that the induced morphism

{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{c}]/{\cal K}_{0}^{e}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}/{\cal K}^{e}
is an isomorphism.
Set
{\cal Q}^{\prime\prime}:={\mathbb{A}}^{c}_{T}
.
Because
p
is locally nilpotent on
T
, we may assume that
there exists a positive integer
e
such that

{\cal K}^{e}{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}=0
.
By
[
BO1
, 3.32 Proposition]
,

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}
is isomorphic to
the PD-polynomial algebra

{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}}\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{c}\rangle
.
Hence we have the following isomorphisms

(4.1.2)

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{i^{\prime}+i^{\prime\prime}=i}\Omega^{i^{\prime}}_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal O}_{T}\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{c}\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\prime\prime}}}\Omega^{i^{\prime\prime}}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\prime\prime}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

and

(4.1.3)

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}P^{M_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}\setminus N_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}_{k}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{i^{\prime}+i^{\prime\prime}=i}P^{M_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}}\setminus N_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}}}_{k}{\Omega}^{i^{\prime}}_{{\cal Q}^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal O}_{T}\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{c}\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\prime\prime}}}\Omega^{i^{\prime\prime}}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\prime\prime}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

Since the complex

{\cal O}_{T}\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{c}\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\prime\prime}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\prime\prime}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

consists of free
{\cal O}_{T}
-modules,
we obtain the desired injectivity.
∎

Set

(4.1.4)

\displaystyle P^{M_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}\setminus N_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}_{k}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/U}):=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}P^{M_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}\setminus N_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}_{k}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/U}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/U})\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}},k\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Let
S
,
((T,{\cal J},\delta),z)
and
T_{0}
be as in the previous section.
Let
\overline{\cal Q}
be a log smooth integral scheme over

\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Let
\overline{g}\colon\overline{\cal Q}\longrightarrow\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
be the structural morphism.
Set
{\cal Q}:=\overline{\cal Q}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}
and

{\cal I}_{\overline{\cal Q}}={\cal I}_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}}{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}
.
The following is a slight generalization of
[
N4
, (1.3.12)]
.

Proposition 4.2
.

(1)
Set

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}):={\cal I}_{\overline{\cal Q}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Then

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\overline{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})
is
a subcomplex of
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\overline{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.

(2)

Let
L_{\overline{\cal Q}}
and
N_{\overline{\cal Q}}
be sub log structures of
M_{\overline{\cal Q}}

such that
M_{\overline{\cal Q}}=L_{\overline{\cal Q}}\oplus_{{\cal O}^{*}_{\overline{\cal Q}}}N_{\overline{\cal Q}}
.
Assume that, for any point

x\in\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}}
,
there exists a basis
\{\overline{m}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}

of
(L_{\overline{\cal Q}}/{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}^{*})_{x}

and a positive integer
e_{i}>0

(1\leq i\leq k)

such that
\prod_{i=1}^{k}\overline{m}{}^{e_{i}}_{i}
is the image of
the generator
of
(\overline{g}{}^{*}(M_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}/{\cal O}^{*}_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}))_{x}

in
(L_{\overline{\cal Q}}/{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}^{*})_{x}
.
Then
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})

is a subcomplex of

P^{M_{\overline{\cal Q}}\setminus L_{\overline{\cal Q}}}_{0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.

(3)
Let the assumption be as in
(2)
.
Let
M_{\cal Q}
and
N_{\cal Q}
be the inverse image of

M_{\overline{\cal Q}}
and
N_{\overline{\cal Q}}
by the closed immersion

{\cal Q}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}
, respectively.
Then the following formula holds
:

(4.2.1)

P^{M_{\cal Q}\setminus N_{\cal Q}}_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}=P^{M_{\overline{\cal Q}}\setminus N_{\overline{\cal Q}}}_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})\quad(k\in{\mathbb{N}}).

(4)
Let the assumptions be as in
(2)
without assuming that

\prod_{i=1}^{k}\overline{m}{}^{e_{i}}_{i}
is the image of
the generator
of
(\overline{g}{}^{*}(M_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}/{\cal O}^{*}_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}))_{x}

in
(M_{\overline{\cal Q}}/{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}^{*})_{x}
.
Let
m_{i}
be a lift of
\overline{m}_{i}
to
M_{\overline{\cal Q},x}

(1\leq i\leq k)
.
Let
\alpha\colon M_{\overline{\cal Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}
be the structural morphism.
Assume furthermore that

\Omega^{1}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}
is locally free
and that
\{d\alpha(m_{i})\}_{i=1}^{k}
is a part of a basis of

\Omega^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Assume also that

(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},L_{\overline{\cal Q}})
is log smooth over
\overset{\circ}{T}
.
Then the following natural morphism

(4.2.2)

\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

is injective.

(5)
Let the assumptions be as in
(2)
and
(4)
.
Then the injective morphism

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

factors through the following injective morphism
:

(4.2.3)

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

Proof.

(We give the proof for the completeness of this paper.)

(1): Let
x
be a point of
\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}}
.
Let
t
be a local section of
M_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}},\overline{g}(x)}
which
gives a generator of
(M_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}/{\cal O}_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}^{*})_{\overline{g}(x)}
.
By abuse of notation, we denote the image of
t

in
{\cal O}_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}},\overline{g}(x)}
by
t
.
The morphism

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}
is flat.
Consequently the natural morphism

{\cal I}_{\overline{\cal Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}
is injective.
Hence the natural morphism

\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})
is injective.
For a local section

\omega\in{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

around
x
,

d(t\omega)=td\log t\wedge\omega+td\omega
.
Hence

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})

is a subcomplex of

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.

(2):
The question is local.
Let
\alpha\colon M_{\overline{\cal Q},x}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q},x}

be the structural morphism.
Let
m_{i}
be a lift of
\overline{m}_{i}
to

M_{\overline{\cal Q},x}
as stated in (4).
Consider a section

d\log m_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge d\log m_{i_{l}}\wedge\omega

(1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{l}\leq k)

with
\omega\in P^{M_{\overline{\cal Q}}\setminus N_{\overline{\cal Q}}}_{0}\Omega^{j}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

(j\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
By the assumption, we may assume that

\prod_{i=1}^{k}\alpha(m_{i}^{e_{i}})=t
.
Then

td\log m_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge d\log m_{i_{l}}\wedge\omega=d\alpha(m_{i_{1}})\wedge\cdots\wedge d\alpha(m_{i_{l}})\wedge\omega^{\prime}

with
\omega^{\prime}\in P^{M_{\overline{\cal Q}}\setminus N_{\overline{\cal Q}}}_{0}\Omega^{j-l}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Hence

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\overline{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})

is a subcomplex of

P^{M_{\overline{\cal Q}}\setminus N_{\overline{\cal Q}}}_{0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.

(3): By (2) the complex
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})

is a subcomplex of
P^{M_{\overline{\cal Q}}\setminus N_{\overline{\cal Q}}}_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
By the definition of

P^{M_{\cal Q}\setminus N_{\cal Q}}_{k}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,
we have the following natural surjective morphism

P^{M_{\overline{\cal Q}}\setminus N_{\overline{\cal Q}}}_{k}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})\longrightarrow P^{M_{\cal Q}\setminus N_{\cal Q}}_{k}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

The following diagram shows that this morphism
is injective:

(4.2.4)

\begin{CD}P^{M_{\overline{\cal Q}}\setminus N_{\overline{\cal Q}}}_{k}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})@>{}>{}>P^{M_{\cal Q}\setminus N_{\cal Q}}_{k}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{}V{\bigcap}V\\
{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})@>{\sim}>{}>{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.\end{CD}

Hence we obtain (
4.2.1
).

(4):
Take a local chart

(\{1\}\longrightarrow{\cal O}^{*}_{T},P\longrightarrow L_{\overline{\cal Q}},\{1\}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}P)
of the morphism

\overline{\cal Q}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}

on a neighborhood of
x
.
Because
(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},L_{\overline{\cal Q}})

is (formally) log smooth over
\overset{\circ}{T}
,
we can take the
P
such that

{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}

is étale over
{\cal O}_{T}[P]
,
in particular, flat over
{\cal O}_{T}[P]
.
Since
P
is integral, any element
a\in P
defines an injective multiplication

a\cdot\colon{\cal O}_{T}[P]\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{T}[P].

Hence the morphism

(4.2.5)

a\cdot\colon{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}

is injective.
We may assume that
m_{i}
is the image of an element of
P

and that

\Omega^{1}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{k}{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}d\alpha(m_{j})\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{l}{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}\eta_{j}\quad(l\in{\mathbb{N}}),

\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{k}{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}d\log m_{j}\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{l}{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}\eta_{j}\quad(l\in{\mathbb{N}})

with
\eta_{j}\in\Omega^{1}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Let
i
be a nonnegative integer and consider a local section

\omega\in\Omega^{i}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Set
\omega_{j}:=d\log m_{j}

(0\leq j\leq k)
and

\omega_{j}:=d\eta_{j-k}

(k+1\leq j\leq k+l)
.
Express the image of
\omega
in

\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

by the following form

\omega=\sum_{j_{1}<\cdots<j_{i}}a_{j_{1}\cdots j_{i}}\omega_{j_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{j_{i}}\quad(a_{j_{1}\cdots j_{i}}\in{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}).

For
j_{1}<\cdots<j_{i}
, let
n:=n(j_{1},\ldots,j_{i})
be an integer
such that
j_{n}\leq k
and
j_{n+1}>k
.
By the definition of
\omega
, we have

a_{j_{1}\cdots j_{i}}=b_{j_{1}\cdots j_{i}}\alpha(m_{j_{1}})\cdots\alpha(m_{j_{n}})

for some
b_{j_{1}\cdots j_{i}}\in{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}}
.
Hence

\omega=\sum_{j_{1}<\cdots<j_{i}}b_{j_{1}\cdots j_{i}}d\alpha(m_{j_{1}})\wedge\cdots\wedge d\alpha(m_{j_{n(j_{1}\cdots j_{i})}})\wedge\omega_{j_{n(j_{1}\cdots j_{i})+1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{j_{i}}

in
\Omega^{i}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Assume that the image of

\omega
in
\Omega^{i}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
is zero.
Then

0=a_{j_{1}\cdots j_{i}}=b_{j_{1}\cdots j_{i}}\alpha(m_{j_{1}})\cdots\alpha(m_{j_{n}})

by the assumption about the locally freeness of

\Omega^{1}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
By the injectivity of the morphism (
4.2.5
),
we see that
b_{j_{1}\cdots j_{i}}=0
. Hence
\omega=0

and
we have shown that the natural morphism

\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

is injective.

(5): By (1) the morphism

{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

is injective.
By (2)
the morphism

{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

factors through the morphism

{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(-\overset{\circ}{\cal Q})\longrightarrow{\rm Im}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}})
.
The target of the last morphism is isomorphic to

\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}},N_{\overline{\cal Q}})/\overset{\circ}{T}}
by (4).
∎

In the following we assume that
(\overset{\circ}{T},{\cal J},\delta)
is a
p
-adic formal PD-scheme.
Let
(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}},D_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal X},{\cal D})

be an exact immersion into a formal SNCL scheme
{\cal X}/S(T)^{\natural}

with a relative SNCD on
{\cal X}/S(T)^{\natural}
such that the immersion

\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
is an isomorphism of
topological spaces.
Let
{\mathfrak{D}}
be the log PD-envelope of this immersion over

(S(T)^{\natural},{\cal J},\delta)
.
We define the following filtrations
on
\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}

as follows (cf.
[
SZ
, (5.4)]
and
[
E
, I (3.1), (3.2)]
).

Let
P
be a filtration on
\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}

defined by the following:

(4.2.6)

P_{k}\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}=\begin{cases}0&(k<0),\\
{\rm Im}(\Omega^{k}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{X}}\Omega^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal X}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}})&(0\leq k\leq i),\\
\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}&(k>i).\end{cases}

Set
(\overset{\circ}{\cal X},\overset{\circ}{\cal D}):=(\overset{\circ}{\cal X},M({\cal D}))
.
Let

(4.2.7)

P^{\cal X}_{k}\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}=\begin{cases}0&(k<0),\\
{\rm Im}(\Omega^{k}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{X}}\Omega^{i-k}_{(\overset{\circ}{\cal X},\overset{\circ}{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}})&(0\leq k\leq i),\\
\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}&(k>i).\end{cases}

Let
P^{\cal D}
be a filtration on

\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/S(T)^{\natural}}

defined by the following:

(4.2.8)

P^{\cal D}_{k}\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/S(T)^{\natural}}=\begin{cases}0&(k<0),\\
{\rm Im}(\Omega^{k}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/S(T)^{\natural}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{X}}\Omega^{i-k}_{{\cal X}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/S(T)^{\natural}})&(0\leq k\leq i),\\
\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/S(T)^{\natural}}&(k>i).\end{cases}

Here note that, in (
4.2.8
), we consider sheaves of differential forms over

S(T)^{\natural}
not over
\overset{\circ}{T}
.
We use the same notations
P^{\cal D}
,
P
and
P^{\cal X}

for the induced filtration
on
{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal X}}\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/S(T)^{\natural}}
,

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal X}}\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}

and
{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal X}}\Omega^{i}_{({\cal X},{\cal D})/\overset{\circ}{T}}

by the filtrations
P^{\cal D}
,
P
and
P^{\cal X}

(
4.2.8
), (
4.2.6
) and (
4.2.7
), respectively.

Let

a^{(l,m)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(l)}_{T_{0}}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(m)}_{T_{0}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X},\quad b^{(l,m)}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{(l)}\cap\overset{\circ}{\cal D}{}^{(m)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal X}

and

c^{(k)}\colon D^{(k)}_{T_{0}}\longrightarrow X_{T_{0}},\quad d^{(k)}\colon{\cal D}^{(k)}\longrightarrow{\cal X}\quad(l,m,k\in{\mathbb{N}})

be natural morphisms.
Set
a^{(l)}:=a^{(l,0)}
and
b^{(l)}:=b^{(l,0)}
.
For subsets
\underline{\lambda}
of
\Lambda
and
\underline{\mu}
of
M
,
let

a_{\underline{\lambda}\underline{\mu}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}T_{0}}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{\mu}T_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}},\quad b_{\underline{\lambda}\underline{\mu}}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\underline{\lambda}}\cap\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\underline{\mu}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal X}

and

c_{\underline{\mu}}\colon\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{\mu}T_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}},\quad d_{\underline{\mu}}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\underline{\mu}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal X}

be the natural closed immersion.

In the following we define orientation sheaves
for
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
and
\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}}
.

Let
E
be a finite set with cardinality
k\geq 0
.
Set
\varpi_{E}:=\bigwedge^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}^{E}
if
k\geq 1

and
\varpi_{E}:={\mathbb{Z}}
if
k=0
(
[
D1
, (3.1.4)]
).

Let
l
be a positive integer and
let
m
be a nonnegative integer.
For simplicity of notation, set

\underline{\lambda}:=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{l-1}\}

and

\underline{\mu}:=\{\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{m-1}\}
.
Let
P
be a point of

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(l)}_{T_{0}}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(m)}_{T_{0}}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}T_{0}},\ldots,\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{l-1}T_{0}}

(resp.
\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu_{0}T_{0}},\ldots,\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu_{m-1}T_{0}}
)
be distinct smooth components of
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}

(resp.
\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
) such that

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{\mu}}

contains
P
.

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}:=\overset{\circ}{X}_{\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{l-1}\}}

and

\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{\mu}}:=\overset{\circ}{D}_{\{\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{m-1}\}}
.
Then the set

E:=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}},\ldots,\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{l-1}}\}

gives an abelian sheaf

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}):=\varpi_{(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{l-1}){\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}):=\bigwedge^{l}{\mathbb{Z}}^{E}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}}

on a local neighborhood of
P
in
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}
;
the set
F:=\{\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu_{1}},\ldots,\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{l-1}}\}

gives an abelian sheaf

\varpi_{\underline{\mu}{\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}):=\varpi_{(\mu_{0}\cdots\mu_{m-1}){\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}):=\bigwedge^{m}{\mathbb{Z}}^{F}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{\mu}}}}

on a local neighborhood of
P

in
\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{\mu}}
.
Set

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}\underline{\mu}{\rm zar}}((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}+\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}})/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}):=\varpi_{(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{l-1}){\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})|_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}\cap{\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{\mu}}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{(\mu_{0}\cdots\mu_{m-1}){\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})|_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}\cap{\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{\mu}}}}.

We denote a local section
of

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}\underline{\mu}{\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})

by
n(\underline{\lambda}\underline{\mu})=n(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{l-1})\otimes(\mu_{0}\cdots\mu_{m-1})
.

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}})
.
The sheaf

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}\underline{\mu}{\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})

is globalized
on
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(l)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(m)}
;
we denote this globalized abelian
sheaf by the same symbol

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}\underline{\mu}{\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})
.
Set

\displaystyle\varpi^{(l,m)}_{\rm zar}((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}+\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}})/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})

\displaystyle:=\bigoplus_{\{\underline{\lambda},\underline{\mu}\}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}\underline{\mu}{\rm zar}}((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}+\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}})/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})

\displaystyle:=\bigoplus_{\{\lambda_{0},\ldots\lambda_{l-1},\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{m-1}\}}\varpi_{(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{l-1};\mu_{0}\cdots\mu_{m-1}){\rm zar}}((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}+\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}})/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}).

The sheaf

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}\underline{\mu}{\rm zar}}((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}+\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}})/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})

extends to an abelian sheaf

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}\underline{\mu}{\rm crys}}((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}+\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}})/\overset{\circ}{T})

in the crystalline topos

((\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(l)}_{T_{0}}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(m)}_{T_{0}})/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys}
,
and

\varpi^{(l,m)}_{\rm zar}((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}+\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}})/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})
extends
to an abelian sheaf

\varpi^{(l,m)}_{{\rm crys}}((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}+\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}})/\overset{\circ}{T})
.
Set
\varpi^{(l)}_{{\rm zar}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}):=\varpi^{(l,0)}_{{\rm zar}}((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}+\overset{\circ}{D}_{T_{0}})/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})

and
